##############################################################################
# E. VERIFIED CALL CHAIN

E. VERIFIED CALL CHAIN: player fire -> aim ray -> bone
   (read directly from the disassembly, not inferred from RTTI)

E.1 fire action 0x10000007 is read in exactly these places
    005B87F0 005B8817 005B8840 005B8C8F  0x5B8760  player fire handler
    005B6B0C                             XControllerMode_Fly_Scan (Mooch fire)
    005BC8F7 005BC9AE 005BCA2D 005BCA75  0x5BC8E0  melee/whip charge machine
    005BCA9D 005BCABF
    005880CD                             XController_GPig, press edge -> 0x42F880
    005434C1                             registers ids 0x10000007 + 0x10000081
                                         on the animator via 0x6285E4

E.2 0x5B8760  __thiscall, called from 005BEAA4 (XControllerMode_GPig_Default)
    edi   = [esp+4Ch]              input manager, PASSED IN as an argument
    [esp+50h]                      player/item, task slots at +0x2C
    eax   = [00912784]+0x674       input device index
    005B87F8 call 00488A70 level   -> state 1
    005B881F call 00488CE0 press   -> state 3
    005B8848 call 00488C00 release -> state 2
    005B889E mov [task+4], state   task = 0x18 bytes, alloc 0x605919, ctor 0x4BA180,
                                   slot [player+0x2C][ [00915624] ]
    005B88B8 [edi+2774/2778/277C]  -> [cmd+04/08/0C]   ray origin
    005B88E7 [edi+2780/2784/2788]  -> [cmd+14/18/1C]   ray direction
    005B8913 [cmd+24] = 0                              "target found" flag
    005B8A3B..005B8AA5             lock-on override: [target+E8..F4] - origin

E.3 0x488770  __thiscall on the input manager (XGamePad): builds the aim ray
    reads global camera matrix 0x0099B410 and projection 0x0099B1D0 (16 dwords each)
    call 0x622E5A                  EX matrix op
    screen size                    word [0099B5EC]+0x0A / +0x0C
    crosshair screen position:
      [this+279C] / [this+27A0]    when [this+278C]==0 and 0x541070 returns true
      [this+1FBC] / [this+1FC0]    otherwise (mouse)
    writes [this+2774..277C] origin, [this+2780..2788] direction
    normalises the direction at 00488A05..00488A1C
    getters: 0x488700 (ret 4, [+1FCC]/[+1FD0]), 0x488720 (ret 8, same pad/mouse pick)

E.4 0x5340A0  animator update: which bone modifiers run, gated by [this+1F8]
    bit 0x20 -> 0x68D9CB
    bit 0x01 -> EXHeadTracking at [this+1D0]: 0x68DC76 set target, 0x6869F9 apply
    bit 0x02 -> [this+1E0] structs of 0xB8 bytes at [this+1E4] -> 0x68A1F5 each
    bit 0x04 -> next block
    EXHeadTracking   vt=008A9D64  ctor 00686A1F, 0068DBF0  body 00686A37..00686E52
    EXSpineModifier  vt=008A9D4C  ctor 006869D7, 00686E7E  body 006869F3..00686F5D

E.5 consequence for co-op
    The input manager is an ARGUMENT of 0x5B8760, and both the action state and the
    aim ray come out of that one object.  A second input-manager instance therefore
    covers fire and aim in one place.  The camera matrix 0x0099B410 read by 0x488770
    is still process-global, so it has to be substituted separately.

E.6 the input manager is XGamePad, and the engine already builds it at run time
    XGamePad  vt=006FA3EC  ctor 004884B3 and 0048B29D  body 00487F50..0067A9C9
    creation site 0045800D (inside XApp):
      00458029 xor edi,edi
      0045802B push edi
      0045802C push 27ACh          <- instance size 0x27AC bytes
      00458031 call 00605919       <- allocator
      00458047 call 0048B290       <- constructor (writes vt 006FA3EC at 0048B29D)
      00458057 call 00487F10       <- register / init, returns bool
    Both the action bit tables and the aim-ray fields (+0x1FBC, +0x2774, +0x2780,
    +0x278C, +0x279C) live inside that 0x27AC block, so a second instance is a
    self-contained per-player input state.  NOT YET TESTED in game.

E.7 body rotation while aiming: 0x5BB1D0, and the single camera handler
    XControllerMode_GPig_Default::Update 0x5BEB60 calls it at 005BEC24.
      0x5BB1D0  ret 8, body 005BB1D0..005BBBA4
      ecx  = the controller mode; [ecx+4] = owner controller, [owner+0x18] = entity
      arg1 = [esp+5Ch] = the pad          <- an ARGUMENT, not the global 0x9905CC
      arg2 = [esp+60h] = owner+0x4C0      task container, task array at +0x2C
      own task: slot [arg2+2Ch][ [0091564C] ], 0x0C bytes, 0x605919 + ctor 0x4BA2A0

    aim entry, press edges, both already hooked
      005BB270 0x488CE0(pad, dev, 10000011h, 1)
      005BB297 0x488CE0(pad, dev, 10000006h, 1)
      005BB2A0 cmp [entity+26E0h], 40050001h        current item type
      005BB2AF 0x5434A0(entity)
      005BB2BF 0x5366C0(entity, 60050008h, 0)       draw weapon
      005BB2C6 if 0x5434A0 returned 0 -> jump straight to the yaw block 005BBA98

    aim hold, NOW HOOKED
      005BB321 0x488B00(pad, dev, 10000006h, 1, float [006F26D8])
      005BB34D 0x488B00(pad, dev, 10000011h, 1, float [006F2754])
      false from both -> the release path at 005BB9FD
      0x488B00 looks like the hooked 0x488DC0 but is a different function AND has a
      different argument order: (device, action, flags, threshold), the flags word
      pushed before the float.  Read from the prologue, lines 176573..176581:
        00488B00 push esi
        00488B01 mov esi,[esp+0Ch]           <- action
        00488B05 test esi,10000000h
        00488B0D mov eax,[esp+10h]           <- flags
        00488B17 test [008FD20Ch],eax
      Prologue 56 8B 74 24 0C is a clean 5-byte detour site.  While it stayed
      unhooked, a remote player's aim branch was decided by the local physical
      mouse instead of the received snapshot.

    there is exactly ONE camera handler in the process
      00515C80 mov eax,[ecx+18h] / test / mov eax,[eax+00000144h] / ret
      so 0x515C80 on ecx = 915738h is [[915738h+18h]+144h], and 915750h is the
      level singleton, written only at 00467CF7 and 00468677.  It is not a
      per-player slot.  Indexed siblings do exist -- 0x515CA0 -> [ecx+idx*4+2Ch],
      0x515CC0 -> [ecx+idx*4+1Ch] -- but 0x5BB1D0 calls the parameterless one.
      0x5B03A0 (kRefreshGPigCamera) can only re-point that single handler's follow
      target; it cannot give P2 a handler of its own.

    the yaw block 005BBA98..005BBBA4 turns the body while aiming
      005BBA9E 0x4C49B0(arg2, 1) -> edi        small state object, bytes +4/+5/+6
      005BBADC [arg2+2Ch][ [00915620] ] != 0 -> [edi+4] = 1
      005BBAF3 0x515C80 on ecx = 915738h       the shared handler, the call takes
      005BBAF8 esi = camera handler            no player and no pad
      005BBB0E 0x4B6F40(esi+498h) == 44110010h and [state+3Ch] > [008B7824]
               -> bl = 1                       camera is actively turning
      005BBB5A ebp = 0x43BCC0(arg2, 1)         the turn task, the same object as
                                               [owner+4ECh][ [009155F4] ] in 0x5B8D60
      bl == 0: 005BBB67 0x52AD20(esi)    -> [turn_task+10h] = SHARED CAMERA YAW,
                                            the body CAN turn
      bl == 1: 005BBB85 0x534F50(entity) -> [turn_task+10h] = own current yaw,
                                            the body CANNOT turn
      005BBB73 0x4B6F40(esi+498h) == 4411000Ch -> [edi+5] = 1

    every float 0x5BB1D0 writes into that shared handler, unconditionally
      +8B4h, +8B8h    aim-assist accumulators   005BB581, 005BB5CB
      +988h..+994h    locked target position    005BB809..005BB821
      +998h           owner yaw from 0x534F50   005BB7E3, 005BBB43
      +99Ch           aim pitch, or zero        005BB9F2, 005BBB52
      The camera reads +998h and +99Ch back at 005206FF / 0052070E.

    turn task field map, task = 0x43BCC0(owner+0x4C0, 1), two writers
      +0x08  movement speed             0x5B8D60
      +0x0C  movement desired yaw       0x5B8D60, every frame
      +0x10  AIM desired yaw            0x5BB1D0, from the shared camera handler
      +0x18  current entity yaw         0x5B8D60
      +0x20  movement desired yaw       0x5B8D60, only when an analog axis moved
             more than [006F645C] or the squared length grew; otherwise the
             previous value is kept
      0x5B8D60 movement yaw = atan2(axis1, axis0) + shared camera yaw, and the
      atan2 is skipped entirely when sqrt(axis0^2+axis1^2) <= [008B7824], in
      which case the desired yaw collapses to the current entity yaw.

    every reader of the shared camera yaw 0x52AD20
      004F4E61 (XGPigScanMode)   00587E59 (XController_GPig)
      005B4E5A 005B521E 005B55C5 (Fly / Mooch controller modes)
      005B8DB7 (0x5B8D60 movement turn)      005B92EA (0x5B92A0)
      005BBB67 (0x5BB1D0 aim turn)           005BDE9A

    consequence for co-op
      Everything the aim state machine reads from the pad is per-player, because
      the pad arrives as an argument.  The camera handler is not, and it is a
      singleton rather than a slot, so both players' Default updates read and
      write one handler.  Two failures follow directly.  P2's tick puts
      0x52AD20(shared handler) -- that is P1's camera yaw -- into
      [turn_task_P2+10h], which is why P2 snaps to arbitrary angles.  And P2's
      tick leaves its own turn magnitude in [state+3Ch] of the 44110010h follow
      state, which is exactly the bl gate, so P1's next frame routes its own yaw
      into [turn_task_P1+10h] and P1's body will not turn.  That is the same class
      of defect the inactive mode 0x5B7D60 had with handler+91Ch/920h.
      Observed in game (user): while P2 is present P1 cannot turn with RMB held;
      turning comes back only when BOTH players draw a weapon, or while ctrl is
      held -- and under ctrl P2 spins to arbitrary angles.  With nobody aiming the
      body turns normally.  P2's weapon TYPE is irrelevant.
      An earlier patch that saved only three floats from +994h did NOT fix this.
      A second patch saving +8B4h/+8B8h, +988h..+99Ch and [state+3Ch] around P2's
      tick and hooking 0x488B00 did NOT fix it either: the user then saw P2's body
      turn towards wherever the camera looks while P1's body stopped turning at
      all, with pitch driven remotely correctly.  E.9 is why.

E.9 0x5BCF30 re-drives the single camera from the GLOBAL pad every frame
    0x5BCF30  __thiscall(mode), no stack args, prologue 83 EC 20 D9 EE (5 bytes)
      one caller only: 005BEBD3, inside XControllerMode_GPig_Default::Update
      0x5BEB60, BEFORE 0x5B92A0 (005BEBF7) and 0x5BB1D0 (005BEC24).
      Tail: 005BD500 mov ecx,[esi+4] / add esp,20h / 005BD506 jmp 0x5B8210,
      where esi = [esp+28h] = the mode and [mode+4] = the controller.

    it selects the state of the ONE shared camera handler
      005BD2FC [handler+9A0h] = 4411000Ch      aim state requested
      005BD38B, 005BD3B6, 005BD3F4            44110010h follow state requested
      005BD4C0 [handler+9A0h] = 44110002h     third variant
      also +9A4h float, +9B3h, +9B4h, +973h
      005BD4E8 0x5B03A0([mode+4])  re-points the follow target at that controller
      005BD4F0 0x5B0620([mode+4])
      005BD4F8 0x5B04F0([mode+4])

    the aim-vs-follow decision ignores the pad argument
      005BD33C 0x488DC0(dev, 10000006h, [00718510], 1)
      005BD36C 0x488DC0(dev, 10000011h, [006F951C], 1)
      both take ecx from the process-global XGamePad [009905CC], NOT from the
      pad that XControllerMode_GPig_Default was called with.  During P2's tick
      the 0x488DC0 hook answers those from the remote snapshot, so P2's tick
      requested the follow state on the shared handler and re-pointed it at P2's
      controller.  On P1's next frame 0x5BB1D0 therefore sees follow with
      [state+3Ch] > [008B7824], bl = 1, and P1's body receives its own yaw.

    0x5B8210, the tail of 0x5BCF30, is __thiscall(controller)
      005B8236 0x488CE0(pad, dev, 10000001h, 4)  press edge, global pad again
      005B8268 0x488A70(pad, dev, 10000001h, 4)  held level
      accumulates / decays a float at [controller+8Ch], ceiling [[00912784]+BF8h]
      005B8364 0x466650(owner+520h, 0, edge, &[controller+8Ch]) after an RTTI walk
      against 0070C064 -- gated by [owner+52Ch] != 0.  Touches no shared handler
      field, but it is part of the same camera pipeline, so the skip covers it.

    current patch, VERIFIED IN GAME by the user (body rotation works for P1 and P2)
      0x5BCF30 is hooked and skipped entirely while remote input is active on the
      thread; P2 owns no camera on this machine.
      0x52AD20 is hooked (9-byte relocation: mov eax,ecx + cmp byte [eax+1AB4h],0,
      because the je at 0052AD29 consumes that cmp's flags) and returns the
      SENDER's camera yaw from the packet while remote input is active, which
      covers every reader listed in E.7 at once.
      The sender publishes its own 0x52AD20 result right after its P1 tick.
      [state+3Ch] of the follow state is forced to zero for P2's tick so the bl
      gate takes 005BBB67 (camera yaw) instead of 005BBB85 (own yaw).
      RefreshCameraForController(P2) was removed from P2's tick, and
      RestorePlayer1CameraTarget() now runs only on the frame that actually ran
      SelectMode(P2, 6100003Bh).
      CoopInput grew 256 -> 264 bytes, so host and client need the same DLL.

E.8 second action namespace 0x4008xxxx is reached from the Default update too
    0x5B92A0, called at 005BEBF7, same (pad, owner+0x4C0) signature, ret 8
      005B92EA global camera yaw -> [esp+80h]
      005B9321 0x48B010(pad, dev, 0, 1) and 005B933E axis 1: both results are
               popped with fstp st(0), i.e. queried for their side effect only
      005B934F task slot [arg2+2Ch][ [00915604] ], 0x44 bytes, ctor 0x4B9C40
      005B93B4 0x4B9210(arg2) -> esi, RTTI-walked against 006FFA90, 0070330C,
               00701EA8 -> a bool in [esp+7Ch]
      005B9429 if [0x40CB70(...)+604h] == 50000003h -> skip
      005B9458 0x48AE10(pad, dev, 4008000Ah, 1, thatBool) -> if true [task+4] = 1
      0x48AE10, 0x48AF10 and 0x48AF90 are the queries for the 0x4008xxxx
      namespace and none of them is hooked yet.  NOT YET TESTED in game.

E.10 how a logical action becomes a physical key -- read fully in 0x488A70
    0x488A70(device, action, flags), __thiscall(XGamePad)
      action in [10000000h, 10000043h) is replaced by
        binding = [this + 2668h + n*4]
      then requires [008FD20C] & flags, then 0x488640(device, binding), then
      dispatches on the binding's high bits:
        binding & 40000000h -> mask test against [[0099B6B0] + 4]
        else binding & 80000000h -> byte [[00AA6580] + 4 + (binding & 0FFh)],
             i.e. the LOW BYTE IS A DIRECTINPUT (DIK) SCAN CODE
        else -> [this + device_byte*8 + 4] & binding   (device button mask)
    0x488640(device, mask)
      device 0 -> idx0, 2 -> idx1, 4 -> idx2, 8 -> idx3, 20h -> idx4
      needs [this+2628h+idx*4] & mask and !(0.0 >= [this+2648h+idx*4])
      counterpart setter for [this+2628h+idx*4] is 0x4886A0
    default-binding initializer 0x48A3A5
      lea esi,[edx+2668h] + mov ecx,43h + rep stos clears all 0x43 entries, then
      branches on [edx + 278Ch]:
        0 -> 0x48A627  pad/mask profile, n=07h fire = 800000h
        1 -> 0x48A509  8000xxxxh keyboard set A, n=02h..04h = 1Eh/1Fh/20h
        2 -> 0x48A3EB  keyboard set B,          n=02h..04h = 25h/26h/27h
      shared tail 0x48A75E: n=32h->29h, 33h->2Fh, 34h->30h, 37h->52h, 38h->51h,
      39h->50h.  Profiles 1 and 2 leave fire (n=07h) unbound, so neither is the
      real PC play profile.
    Q = the fly (Mooch), action index 0Eh -- PROVEN
      0x5BBC80 is the Mooch-switch routine; gate at 005BBCAC is
      0x488CE0(pad, dev, 1000000Eh, 1); it ends 005BBD94 with
      mov eax,[edx+20h] / push 61000065h / call eax.
      The only other 0x0E site is 005B63AC (the Fly controller).
    actions 14h and 16h are XControllerMode_GPig_RDV, NOT night vision
      both queries live in 0x5BD5D0, which is called only from 005BDD5E inside
      the RDV update 0x5BDCD0.
    GPig mode vtables, read out of the EXE .rdata
      0071843C Inactive   61000000h
      007184EC Default    6100003Bh  (slot 3 = Update 0x5BEB60, slot 5 = 0x5BD510)
      007183C4 Scan       6100000Ch
      0071839C Mooch      61000065h
      00718414 Carried    61000052h
      00718524 RDV        6100006Eh  (slot 3 = Update 0x5BDCD0)
      00718464 MiniGame   61000081h
      007183EC Pause      6100009Bh
    night vision has NO semantic action index at all
      XItemHandler_GadgetGoggle vt=00713774, ctor 0x5915D0 (vtable write 00591628),
      constructed at 00543297 with NO 5000xxxxh id init, and the gadget labels in
      the factory 0x5424E0..0x543319 are hashes (04001945h, 04001B0Fh, ...), so
      there is nothing to trace an action back from.  The live binding dump then
      settled it: DIK 0Fh (TAB) is bound to NONE of the 0x43 actions, so TAB is
      read straight out of the DirectInput buffer and only the VK_TAB skip can
      close it.  Confirmed in game: TAB works per-player and blocks nobody.
    current patch, TAB/Q RESULT VERIFIED IN GAME by the user (neither is mirrored
    any more, TAB works per-player); the index-based revision awaits a test
      CoopNetGame::IsMirrorSuppressedAction pins fixed semantic INDEXES -- 09h
      (DIK 10h = Q in the live table, the key that calls the fly) and 0Eh (the
      proven Mooch toggle) -- instead of matching a scan code, because keys are
      rebindable.  No pad read, no binding lookup, so a rebind cannot reopen it.
      Suppression sits on the answering side, not the capture side:
      GetActiveRemoteAction, GetActiveRemoteHold and both edge branches (0x488CE0,
      0x488C00) return false, and the inverse query 0x488B70 therefore answers
      "released".  CoopInput is UNCHANGED at 264 bytes.
      The raw paths are closed too: BuildRemoteScanCodeState never sets VK_TAB or
      'Q' in the swapped DirectInput buffers (+04h/+204h) and
      HandleGetAsyncKeyState returns 0 for them -- this matters because the
      0x4008xxxx family (0x48AE10, E.8) is still unhooked and reads the buffer
      directly, and because that skip is the ONLY mechanism TAB has.
      Reason: night vision and the fly spend a pool owned by the machine the
      action happens on, so replaying the sender's key drained one pool twice.
      OPEN DEFECT, deprioritised by the user ("main thing is it does not block
      the others"): switching to the fly blocks the controls of the player who
      switched.  Local entry into XControllerMode_GPig_Mooch, not mirroring.

    the three 0x48A3A5 profiles are DEVELOPER profiles, decoded (n=(disp-2668h)/4)
      profile 0  0x48A627  pad masks
        n=01h/02h 4000h, 06h 100000h, 09h 80000h, 0Ah 40000h, 0Dh 400000h,
        11h 20000h, 12h 10000h, 15h 400000h, 2Ch 44h, 2Dh 88h
      profile 1  0x48A509  keyboard set A
        00h Z, 01h '7', 02h..04h A/S/D, 06h E0h, 09h Q, 0Bh backslash, 0Dh E1h,
        17h '0', 1Ch '3', 1Dh '6', 1Eh '[', 1Fh U, 3Eh '7', 41h Y, 42h '8'
      profile 2  0x48A3EB  keyboard set B
        00h Z, 02h..04h K/L/semicolon, 06h E6h, 09h Q, 0Dh E5h, 17h '0',
        1Ch backspace, 1Dh comma, 1Eh E, 1Fh TAB, 26h/27h numpad2/1,
        2Ch/2Dh numpad2/1, 41h O
      shared tail 0x48A75E
        33h V, 34h B, 35h apostrophe, 37h numpad0, 39h numpad2, 3Ah numpad1,
        3Bh 40000020h, 3Ch 40000040h
      A/S/D vs K/L/semicolon is a two-players-on-one-keyboard layout and neither
      set binds fire (n=07h), so the shipped PC layout is written elsewhere.
      GForce.ini carries no bindings either -- there is nothing static to read.
    weak corroboration that 0Dh and 0Eh are the pair of special toggles
      in the switch getter 0x489FE0..0x48A250 every case returns the binding value
      [pad + 2668h + n*4] EXCEPT two, which return a literal action id:
      1000000Eh at 0048A019 and 1000000Dh at 0048A0A6.
    0Dh call sites, for reference
      005BBC55  0x488A70 level, inside a 0x0C-byte task builder ending ret 8 at
                005BBC79; sets byte [task+4] = 1
      005BCE24, 005BCEE1  in the function right before the camera update 0x5BCF30
    runtime diagnostic that produced the ground truth
      CoopNetGame::DumpActionBindingsOnce, called from GameTick, writes P1's live
      binding table (n, kind, scan code + key name) and [pad + 278Ch] to
      gforce_coop.log exactly once per session -- 0x43 entries, not a frame log,
      retried only while the pad is still unregistered (0x487F10).  Alongside it
      CaptureLocalPress logs one "[press] first local press n=.." line per action
      index per process (68 lines max), so a single key press names its own index.
    LIVE BINDING TABLE, profile=1, dumped from two launches (pads 02FBA900 /
    02E8A900) -- this is the ground truth, and it does NOT match any static profile
      09h  KEY 10h Q            <- the fly (kFlySummonActionIndex)
      0Eh  KEY 14h T            <- Mooch toggle, proven at 0x5BBC80/0x5BBCAC/
                                   0x5BBD94 (kMoochActionIndex); NOT TAB
      0Fh  KEY GRAVE      10h  KEY APOSTROPHE      3Dh  KEY T
      07h  MASKNS 40000001h     <- fire is the MOUSE, not a key
      13h/16h/19h/36h MASKNS 40000002h, 14h/2Eh MASKNS 40000001h,
      3Bh MASKNS 40000020h, 3Ch MASKNS 40000040h
      keyboard entries also present: 00h Z, 01h '7', 02h A, 03h S, 04h D, 06h E0h,
      0Ah GRAVE, 0Bh backslash, 0Dh E1h, 17h '0', 1Ch '3', 1Dh '6', 1Eh '[',
      1Fh U, 26h/2Ch numpad2, 27h/2Dh numpad1, 28h/35h apostrophe, 29h/32h GRAVE,
      2Ah/2Fh/37h numpad0, 2Bh/30h/38h numpad3, 33h V, 34h B, 39h numpad2,
      3Ah numpad1, 3Eh '7', 41h Y, 42h '8'
      DIK 0Fh (TAB) appears NOWHERE -> night vision is not a logical action.
      Fire arriving as a mouse mask proves the shipped layout is written at runtime
      on top of developer profile 1, which is why no static table could be trusted.
    goggle item handler, for the night-vision hunt
      XItemHandler_GadgetGoggle vt=00713774, ctor 0x5915D0
      slot 5  0x5916D0  mov byte [ecx+658h],1 / jmp 0x45FAA0   <- activate
      slot 78 0x5916B0  mov [ecx+61Ch],4001A7Dh / mov byte [ecx+658h],0  ret 4
      slot 76 0x591680  if [ecx+660h] then [ecx+624h] vcall +10h (61000082h)
      slot 79 0x591750  update, gated on byte [esi+658h] != 0
      [+658h] is therefore the goggles-active flag, but none of these is reached
      by a direct call -- only through the vtable -- so no input site was found
      this way.
